# Requirements And Verification Traceability

## Purpose

This document is the durable bidirectional requirements-to-architecture-to-code-
to-verification matrix. It implements the Requirements Recovery Principle in
`docs/01-product-charter.md` without treating Copperfin's existing behavior as
its own requirement source. The historical filename remains stable because
recovered compatibility requirements are one important subset of this matrix.

Allowed requirement evidence is limited to observed behavior from a real,
installed VFP9 environment, shipped Microsoft/VFP documentation, explicit
repository-owner product policy, and registered known-bug or crash exceptions
in `docs/27-known-vfp9-bug-exceptions.md`.
Decompiled or disassembled VFP binaries are prohibited inputs under
`docs/07-clean-room-rules.md`.

Existing Copperfin implementation and tests may confirm or contradict a
requirement, but may not originate one. A derived requirement must identify its
parent product requirement or hazard. When allowed evidence and implementation
disagree, use `gap` or `exception`; never rewrite the requirement to describe
whatever the code happens to do.

## Assurance Boundary

Copperfin's DO-178C-inspired discipline is adapted to a general-purpose
C++/.NET platform. This matrix is not evidence of formal DO-178C compliance,
an assigned software level, certification, or suitability for a particular
safety-critical use. Integrators retain the responsibilities stated in the
product charter.

Every behavior-changing slice must add or cite a governing row and preserve
both directions of traceability. Higher-hazard or broader-reach work must also
record misuse and boundary analysis, rollback, applicable `HZ-*` controls,
focused and broader verification, retained results, and release-evidence
disposition. Unknown relationships remain explicit gaps rather than inferred
coverage.

## Status Values

- `defined`: an explicit owner product requirement or a derived requirement has
  its allowed source/parent plus mapped architecture, code, tests, and retained
  verification evidence.
- `recovered`: the requirement has allowed source evidence and mapped code and
  test evidence.
- `gap`: allowed source evidence exists, but code or test evidence is missing or
  contradicts the requirement. Track implementation in a separate prompt-sized
  issue.
- `exception`: the behavior is intentionally different and has an applied
  `KBX-*` entry in `docs/27-known-vfp9-bug-exceptions.md`.

| `RQ-CF-PRG-GATHER-001` | Repository-owner-authored, `agent-approved` issue #6320; retained ASan repro cited in the issue (`heap-use-after-free` in `PrgRuntimeSession::Impl::execute_current_statement()` at `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl:10116`, pre-refactor line numbers); derived from `HZ-runtime-crash-01` and `HZ-data-corruption-01` | `GATHER`'s `FOR` predicate and non-bare/macro-expanded `FROM` source-name expression each execute arbitrary VFP code (including nested `EVALUATE()`, which runs at an evaluation depth the direct-call trampoline does not protect) capable of closing or replacing the target cursor via `USE IN`/`CLOSE ALL`, or of switching the selected data session without closing it. `GATHER` shall retain the target's generation identity (data session, work area, binding identity) rather than a raw `CursorState*` across both evaluation points, threading that identity through a suspended-and-resumed direct routine call the same way `GO`/`SKIP`/`SEEK`/record `UNLOCK` (`RQ-CF-PRG-035`) already do. It shall reacquire that exact generation after each evaluation point -- including after a `FOR` predicate that evaluates false -- before any transaction-backup check, record lock, field write, or unlock, and shall fail with the same catchable `{command} target work area not found` error on closure or replacement, before touching a replacement or emitting false success. The transaction-backup check, record lock, field write, and unlock shall run against the target's own origin data session regardless of what the evaluated expression left selected, exactly as `SEEK` already does. | `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`gather_command`: top-of-function `resumed_command_cursor_reference` resolution distinguishing "no current work area" from "cursor lost across a suspended predicate"; a shared `revalidate_cursor` lambda called after the `FOR` predicate, after a false predicate before the "skipped" event, and after `FROM`-source-name resolution; `command_cursor_reference` threaded through every suspension point in this command, including the `FROM`-source name-resolution continuation; a `ScopedDataSessionSelection` pinning the transaction-backup/lock/write/unlock sequence to the cursor's own origin session) | `tests/test_prg_engine_data_io_scatter_gather.cpp` (`test_gather_for_clause_closing_target_cursor_fails_catchably`, `test_gather_for_clause_closing_target_cursor_and_returning_false_fails_catchably`, `test_gather_for_clause_switching_data_session_journals_to_origin_session`, `test_gather_macro_array_name_closing_target_cursor_fails_catchably`) | Focused portable `test_prg_engine_data_io` reproduces the issue's own `EVALUATE("CloseTarget()")` trigger for both true and false predicate outcomes, asserting the specific localized `{command} target work area not found` message (via the active catalog, not a hardcoded literal) rather than just "an error was caught"; a data-session-switching predicate that leaves the cursor open, verified by confirming `UNDO` back in the origin session finds and reverts the journal entry there (proving correct session attribution, not just that the write itself landed); and a macro-expanded `FROM` source name that closes the cursor with no `FOR` clause involved. All four verified fail-then-pass against the reverted fix via a captured-SHA git stash, not just an assertion failure: without the fix, the true-predicate case still throws *some* catchable error, but a generic resource-fault message from dereferencing corrupted freed memory rather than the fix's own check, and the false-predicate case doesn't fail at all -- pinning the exact message closed that gap. `ctest -R "test_prg_engine"` (48 tests) passed after each round. | `HZ-runtime-crash-01`; `HZ-data-corruption-01`; the revalidation added after `FROM`-source-name resolution is presently unreachable defense-in-depth, since every routine call in this engine suspends through the already-threaded `resumed_command_cursor_reference` check rather than completing synchronously -- verified by attempting to isolate it with a fail-then-pass test, which could not be made to fail without it, only without the top-of-function suspension-threading. `LOCATE`'s own `FOR` predicate was observed during this investigation to have no equivalent generation guard at all (matching issue #6242) and is not addressed here. | `recovered` |
| `RQ-CF-PRG-APPEND-FROM-001` | Repository-owner-authored, `agent-approved` issue #6321; retained ordinary/ASan/UBSan evidence cited in the issue (`heap-use-after-free` when `append_blank_record_to_file()` reads a freed destination path, and when `filter_expression_matches_record()`'s `restore_cursor_snapshot()` writes through a freed source reference); derived from `HZ-runtime-crash-01`, `HZ-safety-01`, and `HZ-data-corruption-01` | The native-DBF `APPEND FROM` path shall retain generation identities, not raw `CursorState*`/`&`, for both the destination cursor and an already-open source cursor whose `SET FILTER` expression is evaluated per candidate record -- that expression executes arbitrary VFP code (a UDF doing `USE IN`/`CLOSE ALL`) capable of closing or replacing either binding, or of switching the selected data session without closing anything. After every filter evaluation, both bindings shall be reacquired before the next field write, blank-record append, or loop iteration; closure or replacement of either shall raise the existing catchable `{command} target work area not found` error, leave no partially-appended row, and touch no replacement cursor. The command's own undo-rollback guard shall not dereference the destination cursor if closed during the command it is guarding, and its commit/rollback shall operate against the destination's own origin data session regardless of what a filter callback left selected. The shared `filter_expression_matches_record()` helper, and every one of its callers up to and including their own command dispatch, shall report cursor loss instead of unconditionally continuing to use a cursor reference that may already be dangling. | `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`append_from_command`'s native-DBF branch: `open_source_cursor`/destination `cursor` each captured as a `CursorGenerationReference` before the per-record filter evaluation and reacquired after it, failing catchably on loss of either; `AppendFromCommandUndoGuard` changed from a raw `CursorState&` to a `CursorGenerationReference`, re-resolved in its destructor, skipping the restore -- not the journal rollback -- if the destination is gone, and wrapping its own commit/rollback in a `ScopedDataSessionSelection` pinned to the destination's origin session so a filter callback's `SET DATASESSION` doesn't misdirect which session's journal gets committed; `seek_command`'s dispatch propagates a new `execute_seek()` cursor-loss signal past its own `synchronize_relations_for_parent()` call, raising the same catchable error instead); `src/runtime/prg_engine_records.inl` (`filter_expression_matches_record()`: gained a non-defaulted `bool &cursor_lost` out-parameter, forcing every call site to handle it; `src/runtime/prg_engine_cursor.inl` (the indexed-candidate visibility check inside `seek_in_cursor()` -- the other of the filter helper's two callers, and a gap RQ-CF-PRG-035 explicitly disclosed as unaddressed -- stops the scan on loss and now propagates that loss out through a new `cursor_lost` out-parameter rather than silently absorbing it, since a first review round found the caller chain (`execute_seek()`'s own post-scan cursor reads, then `seek_command`'s `synchronize_relations_for_parent()`) still dereferenced the same freed cursor after a bare "stop the scan" fix); Verified fail-then-pass against the reverted fix: the destination-closure scenario reproduces a genuine `SIGSEGV` (captured via output redirection and exit code 139), matching the issue's own reported crash; the DATASESSION and SEEK-propagation fixes were each verified by temporarily reverting only that specific check and confirming their own dedicated tests fail (a clean assertion failure for the DATASESSION case, a UB-but-non-crashing silent "success" for the SEEK-propagation case, consistent with the same allocator-timing-dependent nature already documented for this bug family in `RQ-CF-PRG-052`) before restoring. `ctest -R "test_prg_engine"` (48 tests) passed after both review rounds. | `HZ-runtime-crash-01`; `HZ-safety-01`; `HZ-data-corruption-01`; this row covers only the native-DBF/local-source-filter path (issue #6321) plus the SEEK-side portion of the shared filter helper's own hazard; `SEEK` still does not surface a catchable error for a filter closing its cursor via any path *other than* the one this fix's regression test exercises (e.g. `LOCATE`-style sequential scanning inside `seek_in_cursor`'s remote-candidate branch was not independently re-audited), and the two other pre-existing, un-audited callers of `seek_in_cursor()` itself (`SET RELATION` child-side auto-seek in `prg_engine_cursor.inl:359`, and `prg_engine_records.inl:1047`) were left at their prior, unreviewed behavior -- out of scope for this issue. After the predicate the target shall be re-resolved by generation identity in its original data session before any append or cursor-state update; loss or a session switch shall raise the existing catchable `{command} target work area not found` error, stop further predicate evaluation, and emit no success event. The shared visibility evaluator shall not pass a freed cursor to the second evaluation of a leading-`&` expression | `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`append_from_command`'s remote branch: one shared `append_remote_row` helper replaces the three duplicated push/predicate/`pop_back` sequences; it exposes the candidate through a generation-keyed `RecordEvaluationOverride`, re-resolves a `CursorGenerationReference` and checks `current_data_session` afterward, and appends only accepted rows); `src/runtime/prg_engine_records.inl` (`evaluate_visibility_expression` re-resolves the cursor between the two passes of a leading-`&` expression and returns false on loss) | `tests/test_prg_engine_sql_cursors_mutation_import_export.cpp` (`test_remote_append_from_for_predicate_closing_target_fails_catchably`: JSON/CSV/DBF sources, true/false predicate results, close-then-reopen ABA replacement, `SET DATASESSION` switch, and leading-`&` macro cases; `test_remote_append_from_for_predicate_error_leaves_no_provisional_row`; `test_remote_append_from_for_predicate_packing_target_keeps_rows_consistent`) | Focused portable `test_prg_engine_sql_cursors_mutation` asserts the localized catchable error text, exactly-once predicate evaluation, continued execution after `TRY`/`CATCH`, closed targets staying closed, surviving/replacement cursors keeping their original rows, and correct row counts after a predicate `PACK`. Verified fail-then-pass against the pre-fix source: the original scenarios miss the error and abort from heap corruption (exit 134); the error case left 4 rows; the session-switch case appended into the switched-away target; the PACK case left 4 rows. A local Clang ASan/UBSan build of the same binary passes with the fix; with only the `evaluate_visibility_expression` change reverted, the leading-`&` case reports `heap-use-after-free` in the second evaluation pass (`capture_cursor_expression_reference` via `evaluate_visibility_expression`). Full `ctest` passes; the PR sanitizer job provides the CI ASan/UBSan run | `HZ-runtime-crash-01`; `HZ-safety-01`; `HZ-data-corruption-01`; companion to `RQ-CF-PRG-APPEND-FROM-001` (#6321, local/native-DBF source-filter path). The check runs after the predicate returns, so it covers any callback kind (UDF, `EVALUATE`/`EXECSCRIPT`, object method, external/COM, future lambda), but only VFP UDF callbacks are exercised by tests. While the predicate runs, `RECNO()`/`RECCOUNT()` on the target reflect the pre-append state, because the candidate is not yet in the cursor; this matches VFP applying `FOR` to the incoming row. The `IN (subquery)` form of the shared evaluator was not re-audited here. Audit of other remote-row mutation sites (`append_blank_record`, `pack_cursor`, synthetic SQL row creation) found no callback between mutation and use | `recovered` |
| `RQ-CF-PRG-APPEND-FROM-FOR-001` | Repository-owner-authored issue #6551 (owner decision 2026-09-24); Copperfin probe (local `APPEND FROM ... FOR` never evaluated FOR and appended every row); real VFP 9.0 SP2 probes (Wine VM, 2026-09-24): DBF source into table and cursor targets, `TYPE CSV` and `TYPE SDF` sources, and non-logical FOR results (character, numeric, `.NULL.` all raise error 1127 after exactly one call with nothing appended) | `APPEND FROM ... FOR` shall honor FOR on every local and remote/SQL-result target path. FOR shall be evaluated once up front against the target's current record, and a non-logical result shall raise catchable error 1127 ("You must use a logical expression with a FOR or WHILE clause.") before any source row is read. For DBF sources, FOR shall then be evaluated per source row with the source table selected (`ALIAS()`, `RECNO()`, `RECCOUNT()`, and fields are the source's), opening the source temporarily when it is not already open and closing it afterwards. For text sources (CSV/`DELIMITED`/`TAB`, `SDF`, `DIF`, `SYLK`, `XLS`, and the `TYPE JSON` extension), each candidate shall be appended to the target first and FOR evaluated on that new record, with a rejected row removed again. Cursor loss or a data-session switch during any evaluation shall raise the existing catchable target-not-found error, leaving no provisional row behind | `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`append_from_command`: up-front validation; `evaluate_append_for_on_target`, `evaluate_append_for_on_source` with temporary selection of the source work area, `TemporaryAppendSource`/`open_append_source` reusing or temporarily opening the source DBF, `reject_local_provisional_record` truncating the rejected record only when a `ProvisionalRecordSnapshot` taken before and after user code ran agree on all of: the target's record count, its row-set serial (`dbf_row_set_serial`, bumped by `note_dbf_row_set_change` after every runtime append/truncate/PACK/ZAP/table (re)creation/field add-drop-alter call, keyed case-insensitively on Windows by `dbf_row_set_key` so a mutation through another spelling of the same DBF path still bumps it), the on-disk file size and modification time, and the candidate record's raw bytes — otherwise failing catchably with `AppendFromTargetReshapedDuringFor` without truncating anything (#6560: the earlier raw-byte comparison alone could mistake a byte-identical pre-existing row for the candidate and truncate it; #6561 review: the serial alone missed a callback that overwrote the table file directly via `COPY FILE`, `STRTOFILE`, or `FWRITE` rather than through a runtime DBF writer, so the snapshot also covers file size/mtime/bytes and fails closed on any difference); the remote `append_remote_row` now appends provisionally and tags the row with `DbfRecord::transient_row_token`, a runtime-only identity that survives `REPLACE`, `PACK`, and renumbering; both local FOR helpers treat a `DATASESSION` switch as target loss); `resources/locales/*/strings.json` (`Runtime.Prg.Dispatch.Error.ForClauseRequiresLogicalExpression`) | `tests/test_prg_engine_data_io_import_export.cpp` (`test_append_from_for_follows_vfp9_semantics_on_local_targets`: DBF source context, CSV provisional rows, error 1127); `tests/test_prg_engine_sql_cursors_mutation_import_export.cpp` (`test_remote_append_from_for_follows_vfp9_semantics`: the same three cases on a SQL-result cursor; the #6322 pack test now expects the extra validation call; PR #6553 review scenarios: a per-row `DATASESSION` switch, a callback `DELETE`+`PACK` before rejecting (now a catchable failure that truncates nothing), a callback that deletes the candidate itself and PACKs with a byte-identical pre-existing row (#6560; that row survives), a callback appending after the candidate, a remote callback that `REPLACE`s the provisional row before rejecting it, and a callback that overwrites the target file out-of-band with a same-length replacement DBF via `COPY FILE` before rejecting (#6561 review; the rejection now fails catchably and the on-disk file is never a truncated hybrid of the two)) | Focused portable `test_prg_engine_data_io` and `test_prg_engine_sql_cursors_mutation` assert the exact per-call `ALIAS()`/`RECNO()`/`RECCOUNT()` log, appended rows, the target staying selected, the temporary source work area being closed, and error 1127 with one call and nothing appended. Against the pre-fix source every new assertion fails: local FOR is ignored (all rows appended, no calls, no 1127), remote FOR ran against the target's unchanged current record (`sqlcust:1/3` for every call), and the raw-copy-overwrite case truncated the replacement file instead of failing. A local Clang ASan/UBSan build of both suites is clean. Full `ctest` passes | `HZ-data-corruption-01`; the up-front validation call is one evaluation per command, so the pass stays O(n); rejection of the last provisional record is O(1) (one stat/read-sized snapshot before and after, then a truncate), with a value search only when the callback reshaped the target. `DIF`, `SYLK`, and `XLS` were not probed individually in VFP9; they follow the probed CSV/SDF text-import behavior. `APPEND FROM ARRAY` has no FOR clause and is unchanged. Any local FOR callback that adds, removes, packs, zaps, rewrites, or out-of-band overwrites target rows and then rejects the candidate fails catchably (`AppendFromTargetReshapedDuringFor`) and may leave the candidate in place; a DBF record has no identity that would locate it safely, and equal bytes are not identity (#6560, #6561) | `recovered` |
| `RQ-CF-PRG-SCAN-001` | Repository-owner-authored, `agent-approved` issue #6331; deterministic out-of-tree PRG harness evidence in the issue (a `SCAN FOR` UDF runs `USE IN Source` then `CREATE CURSOR Replacement`, which reuses work area 1, and the loop body then reads `Replacement.ID=99`) | `SCAN` shall resolve its scanned cursor by generation identity (data session, work area, binding identity), never by bare work-area number, at every resumable predicate/filter boundary and at `ENDSCAN`/`LOOP`. A cursor that was closed, closed and replaced by another cursor reusing the same work area, or left behind by a `DATASESSION` switch shall be treated as lost: the resumable predicate path raises the existing catchable `{command} target work area not found` error, and `ENDSCAN` ends the loop exactly as it already did for a plain close. The replacement cursor shall never be positioned, iterated, or mutated by the loop | `src/runtime/prg_engine.cpp` (`ScanState` and `ScanExpressionContinuation` carry a `CursorGenerationReference`; the struct definition moved ahead of `ScanState`); `src/runtime/prg_engine_flow.inl` (`resolve_scan_cursor` requires the scan's own data session plus a matching binding identity, and replaces the three `find_cursor_by_area` lookups in `continue_scan_loop`, `complete_scan_expression_search`, and `continue_scan_expression_search`; the direct `ENDSCAN` search re-resolves after `locate_next_matching_record`); `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`SCAN` entry captures the reference before either search path and re-resolves after the direct search; the `ON ERROR ... SUPERSEDED CLAUSE (by `RQ-CF-PRG-CLOSE-SCOPE-001`, #6195): `CLOSE ALL`/`CLOSE DATABASE(S)` are no longer a bulk-removal trigger at all -- VFP9 does not release objects on any CLOSE variant, so this mechanism now applies to them only in the sense that a before-handler running `CLOSE ALL` no longer touches the source at all. STILL IN FORCE: session shutdown remains a bulk-removal trigger, and after the method releases its own object (`THIS.Release()` followed by `THIS.member`). The released state is destroyed at the next top-level statement boundary, or when `run()` returns on any terminal path. The choice is not tied to `SET COMPATIBLE` (FoxBASE+/dBASE behavior, #6223); a stricter Copperfin-only mode belongs to #6194 | `src/runtime/prg_engine.cpp` (`park_released_native_object` extracts the `ole_objects` node instead of erasing it at every release site; `park_all_native_objects` replaces the bulk `ole_objects.clear()` in session shutdown -- no longer called from `close_runtime_scope()`, see `RQ-CF-PRG-CLOSE-SCOPE-001`; release no longer nulls the in-flight method's own `THIS` local; `find_parked_native_object`; parked nodes are cleared at the top of every outermost-loop iteration, before the completion check, and in `finalize_pause_state` on every terminal return); `src/runtime/prg_engine_session_native_layout.inl` (third release site); `src/runtime/prg_engine_native_reflection_dispatch.inl` and `src/runtime/prg_engine_event_binding_dispatch.inl` (the #6538/#6540 1924 paths are removed; delegates stay keyed by the stable handle); `src/runtime/prg_engine_session_native_classes.inl` (`THIS` member paths fall back to the parked object) | `tests/test_prg_engine_runtime_surface_functions_interop_04.cpp` (`test_property_read_handler_releasing_source_fails_catchably` and `test_method_before_handler_releasing_source_fails_catchably`, re-pointed at the VFP9 contract: values returned, bodies run, remaining handlers run, release-then-create running the body on the original object via `THIS.cTag`, `THIS.Release()` then `RETURN THIS.cTag`, and a before-handler running `CLOSE ALL` -- since #6195 that scenario now demonstrates the source is never touched by `CLOSE ALL` at all, rather than exercising the parked-object fallback) | Focused portable `test_prg_engine_runtime_surface_functions` passes, and a local Clang ASan/UBSan build of it is clean across every release scenario (before/after handlers, `_Access` self-release, container removal, unbind-then-release, release-then-create). | `RQ-CF-PRG-USE-BARE-TARGET-001` | Repository-owner-approved issue #6557; retained evidence in the issue (standard test harness against a real `people.dbf`: `USE people`, `USE people ALIAS People IN 0`, and `USE people IN 0` all fail with "Unable to resolve USE target: <dir>/.dbf"; `USE people.dbf` faults with "Runtime fault: OLE object not found for property read: people.dbf"; only a quoted literal path opened the table); VFP9's documented `USE` grammar (`FileName \| ? \| (cTableAlias)`, evaluating only a parenthesized expression or a macro) | `USE`'s primary target shall be treated as a literal file/table name -- resolved against the default directory and `SET PATH` exactly like a quoted literal -- whenever the parsed token is not a parenthesized expression, a leading `&` macro, or already single/double-quoted or bracket (`[...]`)-quoted. Only those forms shall be evaluated as a VFP expression, and a parenthesized expression's own embedded spaces (from a multi-token expression such as `(cStem + '.dbf')`) shall not truncate the parsed target. This applies to the primary target only; the existing `ALIAS <name>` and `IN <designator>` clauses are unchanged | `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`use_command`: before evaluating `continuation.target_value`, an unquoted, unparenthesized, non-macro, non-bracket-literal token is wrapped directly with `make_string_value()` instead of calling `evaluate_resumable_expression()`; the existing default-alias-from-stem and file-open logic downstream are unchanged, since they already operated on the resulting string); `src/runtime/prg_engine_helpers.cpp`/`.h` (new `take_first_command_target_token()`: keeps a `[...]` bracket literal or a `(...)` parenthesized expression -- balanced across nested parens and any `'...'`/`"..."` literals inside it -- intact even with embedded spaces, instead of the general-purpose `take_first_token()`'s space-delimited truncation); `src/runtime/prg_engine_parser.cpp` (`USE` parsing calls the new tokenizer instead of `take_first_token()` for the primary target) | `tests/test_prg_engine_work_areas.cpp` (`test_use_bare_target_name_resolves_as_literal_filename`: `USE people` with a same-named PUBLIC variable holding an unrelated string (proving the bare token is not evaluated), `USE people.dbf ALIAS PeopleTwo`, and `USE cities IN 0`; `test_use_bracket_literal_and_spaced_parenthesized_target_resolve_correctly` (PR #6570 review): `USE [people.dbf]` and `USE (cStem + '.dbf')`; `test_use_target_expression_uses_heap_backed_frame_continuations`'s two scripts updated from a bare `use_target(2048)`/`use_target()` call to an explicitly parenthesized `(use_target(2048))`/`(use_target())`, since a bare function-call-shaped token is no longer evaluated either -- matching real VFP9's own `USE (expr)` grammar rather than the pre-fix accidental behavior that test relied on) | Focused portable `test_prg_engine_work_areas` asserts `RECCOUNT()`/`ALIAS()` for all five scenarios. | `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-001` | Explicit repository-owner migration policy in `docs/03-compatibility-and-migration.md` (import-pipeline scan/inventory and `inventory.json` output); derived safety constraints from `HZ-system-failure-01` and `HZ-data-corruption-01` | The migration foundation shall accept only an absolute project-root directory that is not itself a symbolic link, enumerate its regular-file metadata without reading contents, classify known VFP asset suffixes, return project-relative entries in deterministic lexical order, and serialize schema-version-1 inventory JSON without the absolute root. It shall not follow a file or directory symbolic link or a Windows reparse point; it shall report skipped project-relative indirect-entry names through the existing `skippedSymlinks` field. Invalid/unavailable roots, unrepresentable path names, and traversal errors shall return a stable incomplete result. The boundary shall not parse, execute, upload, rename, delete, write, or otherwise mutate a project asset. | `include/copperfin/migration/project_inventory.h`; `src/migration/project_inventory.cpp`; `cf_migration` | `tests/test_project_inventory.cpp`; broader portable/platform validation required before release evidence | Focused Debug build and CTest pass `1/1` on Linux; direct coverage proves deterministic order, suffix classification, JSON escaping, absolute-root omission, invalid-root and non-UTF-8 POSIX-name denial, and file/directory symlink non-traversal. Protected Windows validation is required to prove directory-junction/reparse-point non-traversal. | `HZ-system-failure-01`; `HZ-data-corruption-01`; no asset-content parsing, dependency analysis, source mutation, risk scoring, migration-mode selection, database mapping, or other first-tool output is implemented | `defined` |
| `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-002` | Repository-owner #5521 prerequisite slice for #5476 (parent #141); evidence source is **community reverse-engineering documentation, not an official Microsoft specification or independently observed real-Access-file behavior** -- `docs/66-access-container-format-notes.md` (issue #5474) found no official Microsoft spec exists for the physical MDB/ACCDB byte layout, and `docs/68-access-mdb-jet-physical-page-layout-notes.md` grounds this slice's narrower container-signature scope in the mdbtools project's own `HACKING.md`, cross-checked against independent secondary community sources | The container prober shall recognize an MDB/ACCDB file from its content, not its extension: leading bytes `0x00 0x01 0x00 0x00` followed by the ASCII signature `Standard Jet DB` (Jet engine, conventionally `.mdb`) or `Standard ACE DB` (ACE engine, conventionally `.accdb`) at byte offset 4. It shall read the single engine-generation byte at offset `0x14` and classify it only into the well-corroborated coarse buckets `jet3` (`0x00`) and `jet4` (`0x01`); any value `0x02` or above shall classify as an opaque `later` bucket with the raw byte preserved, since independent sources disagree on the precise year/edition mapping for those values. A file missing the leading bytes/signature, or truncated before offset `0x14`, shall fail closed with a structured error rather than a guessed classification. This is container-level classification only -- no page, table, row, or column decoding is implemented or claimed by this requirement. | `include/copperfin/vfp/access_container.h`; `src/vfp/access_container.cpp` | `tests/test_vfp_assets.cpp` (`test_parse_access_container_header_for_jet3_mdb`, `test_parse_access_container_header_for_jet4_mdb`, `test_parse_access_container_header_for_ace_accdb`, `test_parse_access_container_header_rejects_missing_signature`, `test_parse_access_container_header_rejects_truncated_file`, `test_access_container_errors_resolve_through_localization_catalog`) | Focused tests cover a synthetic Jet 3 MDB header, a synthetic Jet 4 MDB header, a synthetic ACE ACCDB header whose generation byte (`0x02`) correctly lands in the coarse `later` bucket rather than a specific edition claim, a file with an unrecognized signature failing closed, and a file truncated before the generation byte failing closed. Full protected platform validation remains required. | `HZ-data-corruption-01`; container-level signature/generation classification only. No page/table/row/column enumeration (tracked as #5476, intentionally left for a slice with real Access-produced fixtures or materially more careful human-reviewed synthetic-fixture validation, since the row-decode path has no independently-checkable invariant the way this slice's fixed signature bytes and small well-agreed version range do); no JET-vs-ACE encryption/compression handling; no forms/reports/queries/VBA (#138); no writing/mutation of any kind. No real Access-produced `.mdb`/`.accdb` file was available in this environment; both fixtures are documentation-grounded synthetic headers. | `defined` |
| `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-003` | Repository-owner #5523/#5525 first slices of #5517's `IMPORT DATABASE ... TYPE XBASE` wizard (parent #137); reuses #5482/#5483's dBASE/FoxBASE/FoxPro-family readers (`RQ-CF-LEGACY-002`, `RQ-CF-LEGACY-003`) as its read-side ground truth and the existing VFP-native `create_dbf_table_file()`/`replace_record_field_value()` writers as its write-side ground truth | The importer (`import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native()`) shall read one `DbfFormatFamily::dbase`/`foxbase`/`foxpro` source table (any dBASE III/IV/Level-7 version byte, or a FoxBASE/FoxPro version byte) and write a brand-new VFP-native table with equivalent field/record content, per the written mapping table in `docs/69-dbase-import-field-mapping.md`: `C`/`N`/`F`/`L`/`D` copy directly; `M` (memo) copies its decoded text via a second pass through the existing `replace_record_field_value()` REPLACE path, since the base creation API only accepts a blank memo pointer at creation time; `I`/`+` (dBASE Level 7 Long/Autoincrement) map to VFP `I` by value only, not autoincrement behavior; `O` (dBASE Level 7 double) maps to VFP `B` by value. Any other source field type (`B` binary DBT payloads, `@` Julian-day timestamps, or anything else) shall reject the entire import before any destination file is created, rather than silently dropping or corrupting that field. The source table shall be opened read-only and never modified. The destination path shall not be overwritten if it already exists, including its memo-sidecar companion when the target schema has a memo field. Only `code_page_mark == 0` (UTF-8) source tables shall be imported, since a real single-byte code page can expand to a wider UTF-8 byte width the destination's unchanged field width cannot hold. A source memo pointer that could not be resolved to real content (the reader's `"<memo block N>"` diagnostic placeholder) shall reject the whole import rather than write that diagnostic text as content. A failure partway through filling in memo content shall remove the partially-created destination artifacts rather than leave a partial table behind. | `include/copperfin/vfp/dbf_import.h`; `src/vfp/dbf_import.cpp`; `docs/69-dbase-import-field-mapping.md` | `tests/test_dbf_table.cpp` (`test_import_dbase_table_to_vfp_native_round_trips`, `test_import_dbase_table_to_vfp_native_rejects_unsupported_field_type`, `test_import_dbase_table_to_vfp_native_rejects_existing_destination`, `test_import_dbase_table_to_vfp_native_maps_float_to_numeric`, `test_import_dbase_table_to_vfp_native_rejects_non_default_code_page`, `test_import_dbase_table_to_vfp_native_rejects_unresolved_memo_payload`, `test_import_dbase_table_to_vfp_native_rejects_existing_memo_sidecar_conflict`, `test_double_field_round_trips_full_ieee754_precision`, `test_import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native_round_trips_foxbase`, `test_import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native_rejects_foxpro_field_with_non_utf8_bytes`, `test_import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native_round_trips_synthetic_foxpro`, `test_import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native_round_trips_0xfb_foxbase`, `test_import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native_rejects_foxpro_general_field`, `test_import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native_round_trips_clipper_compatible_source`, `test_preview_xbase_table_import_reports_clean_source`, `test_preview_xbase_table_import_reports_all_unmappable_fields`, `test_preview_xbase_table_import_reports_table_level_errors`, `test_preview_xbase_table_import_reports_unresolved_memo_payload`, `test_preview_xbase_table_import_reports_record_value_too_wide`, `test_import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native_preserves_deleted_record_flag`) | Focused tests prove a real 67-record dBASE III + memo fixture (`dbase_83.dbf`) imports into a VFP-native table Copperfin's own reader classifies as `visual_foxpro`, with matching character-field and memo-field content; that a synthetic source containing a `B`-type field is rejected before any destination file is created; and that importing into an already-existing destination path is rejected without touching that file's existing bytes. Further review-driven tests prove a dBASE Float field maps to a genuinely Numeric-typed destination field, a non-default-code-page source is rejected before any destination file is created, a source memo pointer that cannot be resolved to real content is rejected rather than written as a diagnostic placeholder, an existing memo-sidecar companion is detected and rejected even when the primary destination path is free, and a double value needing full IEEE-754 precision round-trips exactly rather than being silently rounded. #5525 extends coverage to FoxBASE/FoxPro sources: a real 9-record FoxBASE fixture round-trips its numeric/character fields; a real historical FoxPro fixture with an actual non-UTF-8-safe field (despite a code-page-0 header) is proven to fail closed via the same width-mismatch detection rather than corrupt the field, since `code_page_mark == 0` is Copperfin's own interpretation, not a guarantee about the original file's real encoding; and a clean synthetic FoxPro (version-byte-patched VFP-native) fixture proves the positive round-trip path for that family. Review-driven tests further prove a FoxPro source containing a General (`G`) field is rejected rather than silently mismapped. #5528 extends coverage to `0xFB`: after research (`docs/70-foxbase-0xfb-investigation.md`) found the "0xFB = FoxBASE" claim traces to an acknowledged-unknown community table entry with no real specimen ever documented, a synthetic `0xFB` source now imports successfully using the same layout as `0x03`. #5530 proves Clipper-compatible sources already succeed with no new code, since Clipper's default DBF/DBT output is byte-identical to dBASE III's and already classifies as `dbase`. #5532 adds `preview_xbase_table_import()`, a dry-run mode sharing the committing path's validation logic (`plan_field_mapping()`) but collecting every unmappable field instead of failing closed on the first, proven by a synthetic two-field source where both fields (`B` and `@`) are reported rather than just the first. Review-driven fixes extended the preview to also catch an unresolved memo payload and a record value that would not fit its destination field once re-encoded (mirroring `write_field_bytes()`'s own 'C'-field width check) -- both conditions the preview originally missed, letting it claim `ok=true` for sources the committing path is guaranteed to reject; proven with a synthetic unresolved-memo fixture and the same real `dbase_f5.dbf` FoxPro fixture whose non-UTF-8-safe `COMN` field already demonstrates the committing path's own rejection. Full protected platform validation remains required. #5567 fixed the previously-documented deleted-record-flag exclusion: `import_xbase_table_to_vfp_native()` now carries the source's own deletion flags through in the same single-pass `create_dbf_table_file_with_deleted_flags()` call (a review-driven fix replacing an initial per-record `set_record_deleted_flag()` loop, which reread and rewrote the entire destination file per deleted record -- quadratic I/O for a deleted-heavy source), proven by a real dBASE III fixture with a byte-level-patched deletion marker (`set_record_deleted_flag()` itself refuses to mutate a legacy dBASE-family file) importing with its deleted/active row set preserved exactly. | `HZ-data-corruption-01`; single dBASE/FoxBASE/FoxPro-family source table only -- no NTX index import (the importer doesn't handle indexes for any source family yet), no DBC container import, and no `B`/`@`/`G`/`P` field import (see `docs/69` for why each is deferred rather than attempted). FoxBASE `0xFB` sources are accepted using the `0x03`-equivalent layout per #5528's research (`docs/70-foxbase-0xfb-investigation.md`) -- a documented best-effort choice, not a verified fact. A code-page-0-marked source is not guaranteed to actually contain valid-UTF-8 bytes for real legacy data (see `docs/69`'s #5525 finding); such fields fail closed via the writer's own width-mismatch detection rather than being silently truncated or corrupted, but are not yet importable. Destination-existence check is a non-atomic `fs::exists()` probe, matching this table-creation family's existing behavior elsewhere in the codebase (unlike #5485's atomic-exclusive writer). | `defined` |

| `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-004` | Repository-owner #5476 first schema-reading slice (parent #141), building on `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-002`'s container-level detector; evidence source is **community reverse-engineering documentation (mdbtools' `HACKING.md`), independently cross-checked against real Jet3 and Jet4 `.mdb` fixtures during this slice's development** -- see `docs/71-access-table-definition-schema-inspection.md` for the full evidence trail, still not verification against an official specification or a licensed Access installation writing the tested bytes itself | Copperfin shall parse a single Table Definition (TDEF) page (page-type byte `0x02`) already loaded into memory, for a container whose generation `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-002`'s detector has already classified: `parse_access_table_definition_page()` shall reject a page whose size does not match its generation's page size (2048 bytes Jet3, 4096 bytes Jet4/ACE -- the ACE figure is an extrapolation from Jet4, not independently verified against a real `.accdb` fixture), whose leading byte is not `0x02`, or whose `next_pg` field is nonzero (a table definition spanning more than one page, explicitly unsupported by this slice), and shall otherwise decode the table's `is_system_table` classification (`table_type` byte `0x53` vs `0x4E`), `row_count`, and every column's name, raw and best-effort-classified type, declared length, and fixed-vs-variable-length classification, using the generation-specific Jet3 (35-byte table-definition block, 18-byte column-descriptor stride, ASCII column names) or Jet4 (55-byte block, 25-byte stride, UCS-2LE column names) layout. Every multi-byte read shall be bounds-checked against the page buffer, failing closed with a structured error rather than reading past it for a page that declares more columns or index entries than it actually has room for. `scan_access_container_schema()` shall discover every TDEF page in a container that is not a continuation page of another TDEF's `next_pg` chain, parse each one, and collect any per-page failure into a `skipped` list rather than aborting the whole scan or silently dropping it; a discovered table's *base* identifier is its TDEF page number, since a table's *name* is not stored in its own TDEF page (it lives in rows of the `MSysObjects` system catalog table, whose row-level decoding this slice alone does not implement); `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-005`'s catalog join (see the hazards column) attaches a real name to each discovered table where that join can independently corroborate one. Index metadata within the TDEF page (the logical index list, index columns, foreign-key relationships) is read past (skipped) without being decoded, since this slice's scope is table/column schema only. | `include/copperfin/vfp/access_table_definition.h`; `src/vfp/access_table_definition.cpp`; `docs/71-access-table-definition-schema-inspection.md`; `resources/locales/*/strings.json` | `tests/test_vfp_assets.cpp` (`test_parse_access_table_definition_page_decodes_jet3_columns`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_decodes_jet4_columns`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_wrong_page_size`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_non_tdef_page`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_multi_page_tdef`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_structure_out_of_bounds`, `test_scan_access_container_schema_discovers_tables_and_skips_continuations`, `test_scan_access_container_schema_rejects_non_access_file`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_oversized_index_count`, `test_scan_access_container_schema_rejects_truncated_trailing_page`, `test_scan_access_container_schema_excludes_every_page_in_a_multi_hop_chain`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_sanitizes_invalid_jet3_utf8`, `test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_unrecognized_table_type`) | Focused tests prove a synthetic Jet3 TDEF page (built from the real-fixture-verified layout, not a committed real file) decodes its system-table classification, row count, and a fixed-length/variable-length/fixed-length column trio's name/type/length correctly; the same for a synthetic Jet4 TDEF page, including UCS-2LE column-name round-tripping; that a wrong-size page, a non-TDEF page, a multi-page (`next_pg != 0`) TDEF, and an inflated declared column count (which would otherwise read past the page) are each rejected rather than misparsed; and that `scan_access_container_schema()` discovers exactly the genuine single-page TDEF in a synthetic multi-page container while reporting a separately-crafted multi-page TDEF chain-start in `skipped` (identified by its own chain-start page, not the continuation page it points at) rather than silently dropping it or misidentifying the continuation page as its own table. A separate non-Access file is proven to be rejected before any page is scanned. Review-driven fixes proved three more cases: (`test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_oversized_index_count`) an index-entry count that could not possibly fit in the page (`0xFFFFFFFF`) fails closed rather than letting `count * stride` overflow and wrap around to a small value that would silently bypass the bounds check meant to catch it -- `PageCursor::skip_repeated()` now divides remaining capacity by stride instead of multiplying count by stride, which cannot overflow; (`test_scan_access_container_schema_rejects_truncated_trailing_page`) a file size that is not an exact multiple of the generation's page size is rejected rather than silently truncated via integer division, which could otherwise leave a real trailing page unexamined with no indication anything was skipped; (`test_scan_access_container_schema_excludes_every_page_in_a_multi_hop_chain`) a three-page TDEF continuation chain (A→B→C) has every page excluded from the discovered-table list, not just the first hop -- the chain's own end page C, whose `next_pg` is 0, would otherwise be misreported as its own independent, successfully-parsed table if the walk stopped after one hop, since nothing about C's own bytes distinguishes it from a genuine single-page TDEF. A second review pass caught two more real correctness gaps, independently proven by two further synthetic tests: (`test_parse_access_table_definition_page_sanitizes_invalid_jet3_utf8`) a Jet3 column name containing a byte that is not valid UTF-8 on its own (a lone `0xC9`, e.g. 'É' in Windows-1252 -- Jet3 names are stored in the database's legacy code page, which this slice cannot yet read without decrypting the RC4-obscured Database Definition page) is replaced with U+FFFD rather than returned as invalid UTF-8; (`test_parse_access_table_definition_page_rejects_unrecognized_table_type`) a `table_type` byte other than the two documented values (`0x4E`/`0x53`) is rejected rather than silently defaulting to "user table," which would otherwise let a corrupt or falsely-detected TDEF page report `ok=true` with fabricated schema data. The same pass also found `scan_access_container_schema()` buffered the entire file into memory before examining any page -- a real Access database can be up to ~2 GB (the Jet/ACE file-size ceiling) -- and restructured it into a two-pass streaming scan (an 8-byte-per-page header probe to discover TDEF pages/continuation chains, then one full page_size buffer reused per page actually decoded), re-verified against the same real Jet3/Jet4 fixtures with unchanged results (table counts identical before and after). Full protected platform validation remains required. | `HZ-data-corruption-01`; table *names* require decoding `MSysObjects`'s rows via the general Jet data-row algorithm, which this slice alone does not implement -- **now satisfied by `RQ-CF-MIGRATION-005` (#5539)**, whose `scan_access_container_schema()` catalog-join attaches real names to the tables this slice discovers, independently cross-checked (that row's own evidence column) against real Jet3 and Jet4 fixtures via the `mdbtools` reader as ground truth. The PowerShell host itself shall be authorized by physical location via `authorize_external_process()`, using an *independently* supplied allowed root (`AccessSaveAsTextExportRequest::powershell_allowed_root`), never derived from the executable path being checked itself (#5562 review, chatgpt-codex-connector and copilot-pull-request-reviewer, independently: deriving the root from the same candidate path makes the containment check tautological, admitting any caller-supplied executable as "inside its own directory") -- deliberately *not* digest-pinned, since it is a well-known OS-shipped executable that changes with every Windows/PowerShell servicing update, unlike the script. The script itself shall be admitted beneath one explicit physical root with its pinned lowercase SHA-256 digest via `admit_polyglot_supporting_artifact()`, sourced out-of-band by the caller (never computed from the file being checked, which would defeat pinning against a tampered substitute). Both admissions shall be revalidated immediately beside the owned launch call. The script's own admitted, revalidated resolved path shall land at a fixed position in a literal argument vector (`-File <path> -DatabasePath <path> -OutputDirectory <path>`, plus `-NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass`), never appended or interpolated from caller-controlled text at any other position. The child process shall receive a complete explicit environment (on Windows, only the `SystemRoot` PowerShell's own host loader requires, sourced from the OS API, never inherited ambient host/agent variables) and a working directory that always exists (the script's own admitted root, not the output directory the script itself is responsible for creating only after its own database-path existence check passes -- creating it eagerly here first would silently change that observable behavior). On completion, `manifest.json` shall be read up to (not beyond) `parse_json_document()`'s own 1 MiB default document-size limit, checked from the file's own size before any read, failing closed as a distinct error rather than allocating an oversized/malformed document in full first (#5562 review, copilot-pull-request-reviewer); a leading UTF-8 byte-order mark shall be stripped before parsing, since Windows PowerShell 5.1's own `Out-File -Encoding utf8` -- the encoding the checked-in script actually uses -- always writes one, and a BOM is not valid JSON (#5562 review, chatgpt-codex-connector and copilot-pull-request-reviewer, independently; this specific defect escaped this slice's own initial testing because its PowerShell 7 test environment's own `-Encoding utf8` does not write a BOM, directly confirmed empirically once flagged). The resulting JSON array of `{Kind, Name, File, Ok, Error}` objects shall be parsed into a structured result whenever manifest.json exists, even on a partial-failure exit code, so a caller can see exactly which object(s) failed; a process that exits non-zero before ever writing a manifest (the source database path did not exist) shall fail closed as a distinct, more specific error than a generic process failure. Every path field in the request shall be required absolute -- a relative `source_database_path`/`output_directory` would resolve against the child process's own working directory (`script_allowed_root`) while this function's own later manifest read resolves a relative `output_directory` against this process's own current directory instead, letting a relative request silently write to one location and read back from another (#5562 review, copilot-pull-request-reviewer). | `include/copperfin/vfp/access_saveastext_export.h`; No expression operands, `IMPORT DATABASE`, SQL/Access/provider adapter, external connection, schema mutation, atomic replace/rollback, or database reconstruction is implemented or claimed. A destination resolving to the same file as the source is rejected at the shared dispatch layer before any read/write, added alongside `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-006` and covering this `TYPE JSON` path too -- see that row for the full description. #5697 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass): a cataloged member DBF whose field-descriptor block parses "successfully" with zero fields (a real, accepted shape) previously emitted an ambiguous `"fields": []` marker and still reported `ok=true` -- see `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-003`'s row for the full description of this fix, shared by every `EXPORT DATABASE` variant. #5630/#5571 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass): a numeric cell's decoded display_value was inserted directly into the JSON stream with no validation at all -- since this codebase's own DBF writer accepts any byte string that fits a fixed-width N/F field's declared width, a crafted or corrupted table could inject an entirely new, distinct JSON property into a record object (e.g. a cell containing `1,"INJECT":true` became two logical properties, not one, while the export still reported success), and a numeric-overflow marker or a nonfinite binary Double's own decoded text ("nan"/"inf") produced outright invalid JSON. Fixed by adding `looks_like_safe_unquoted_json_numeric_literal()`, a JSON-number-grammar validator distinct from (and stricter than) the existing SQL-literal validator -- real JSON grammar (RFC 8259) forbids a leading `+` and a leading zero before further digits, both of which the SQL validator deliberately accepts -- checked before a numeric value is ever emitted unquoted, failing the whole export closed with a new localized `Vfp.AssetInspector.Validation.UnsafeJsonNumericValue` diagnostic naming the table, row, and column. A proactive sibling-gap check (performed while fixing the reported numeric-value defect, not in response to a separate report) found the identical unescaped-raw-byte pattern one property over: the fields array's own `"type"` property embedded a field descriptor's raw type byte directly, with no validation that it's a recognized VFP type letter, allowing a crafted `"` or `\` byte to break the fields array's own JSON structure the same way; fixed by routing it through the same `json_escape_str()` the field's own `"name"` property already uses. New regression tests (`test_export_database_as_json_fails_closed_on_numeric_structural_injection`, `test_export_database_as_json_fails_closed_on_unsafe_numeric_forms`, `test_export_database_as_json_still_accepts_valid_numeric_forms`, `test_export_database_as_json_escapes_crafted_field_type_byte`) verified to reliably fail against the pre-fix code (8 total failures) and reliably pass against the fix. A PR review round (chatgpt-codex-connector) caught that `json_escape_str()`'s own quote/backslash/C0-control-only escaping still let a raw field-type byte >= 0x80 pass through unescaped, producing syntactically valid JSON that is not valid UTF-8 text (which `parse_json_document()` itself rejects); fixed by additionally failing the whole export closed for a type byte outside the printable ASCII range, with a new `Vfp.AssetInspector.Validation.UnsafeJsonFieldTypeByte` diagnostic, proven by a new `test_export_database_as_json_fails_closed_on_non_ascii_field_type_byte` regression test. #5743 (found by the same review pass, a sibling gap to #5630/#5571's own field-TYPE-byte fix): DBF field-descriptor NAME bytes are read raw by `read_ascii_name()` (`dbf_table.cpp`) with no charset validation, and every exporter -- JSON and all five SQL dialects (portable SQL/PostgreSQL, SQL Server, Oracle, Access, MySQL) -- trusted them as already-valid UTF-8; a crafted or corrupted name containing an isolated high byte reached JSON output verbatim (producing text no conforming UTF-8-based JSON parser can accept) and reached every SQL dialect's identifier quoting the same way. Fixed by adding a new `is_valid_utf8()` validator and checking every field name against it before it is ever quoted or escaped, failing the whole export closed with a new `Vfp.AssetInspector.Validation.UnsafeFieldNameBytes` diagnostic if it is not valid UTF-8 -- one check point per writer's own per-field loop, which by construction also covers every record's own field-name-keyed property/column, since `DbfTableParseResult` copies each record value's `field_name` directly from the same field descriptor list. Full codepage-aware conversion of legacy-encoded names to Unicode (this issue's own broader completion criteria) is not attempted here; this closes the immediate correctness/safety gap only. New regression tests (`test_export_database_family_fails_closed_on_invalid_utf8_field_name`, covering all six export dialects; `test_export_database_family_still_accepts_valid_multibyte_utf8_field_name`, proving a genuine multi-byte UTF-8 name is not rejected) verified to reliably fail against the pre-fix code (7 of 8 assertions; Oracle's own pre-existing identifier-collision logic happened to already reject this exact crafted input for an unrelated reason) and reliably pass against the fix. #5817 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass running the full Clang ASan/UBSan and ThreadSanitizer suites; a regression introduced by closed #5636's own containment fix, PR #5685): `load_database_catalog_snapshot()` -- shared by every JSON and SQL-family exporter -- held `tname` as a `const std::string&` referencing a string stored inside `snapshot.catalog`, then in all three rejection branches (unsafe table name, table path escaping the database directory, memo-sidecar path escaping the database directory) reset the whole snapshot via `snapshot = {}` -- destroying the catalog vector `tname` referenced into -- before reading `tname` again to format the rejection diagnostic, a heap-use-after-free on every crafted DBC these branches exist to reject. The ordinary GCC Release ctest suite passed because the freed heap storage happened to remain readable; only a sanitizer build actually caught it. Fixed by making `tname` an owning `const std::string` copy instead of a reference, decoupling its lifetime from the snapshot being reset -- a one-line change covering all three branches, since they share the same `tname` declared once per loop iteration. Independently reproduced with a standalone Clang ASan/UBSan build of `test_vfp_assets` against the existing `test_export_database_as_json_rejects_dotdot_table_name_traversal` regression (clean heap-use-after-free abort at the exact reported line, matching Codex's own report) before the fix, and confirmed the full suite passes clean under the same sanitizer build after it; no new regression test was needed since all four existing containment-rejection tests (`..._rejects_dotdot_table_name_traversal`, `..._rejects_absolute_table_name`, `..._rejects_symlink_table_escaping_directory`, `..._rejects_symlink_memo_sidecar_escaping_directory`) already exercise this exact code path and now serve as the sanitizer regression guard. Adding permanent ASan/UBSan/TSan coverage to CI for this containment-rejection test family (this issue's own broader completion criteria) was not attempted here -- scoped to the memory-safety fix itself. #5796/#5797/#5798 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass, three distinct defects in `decode_dbc_properties_blob()`'s own PROPERTIES-memo binary decoder): (1) #5796, a DateTime property (type `0x05`) was emitted as raw hex "pending full decode" rather than a real decoded value, despite the public `DbcProperty` contract's own claim of decoded, round-trippable values; (2) #5797, the decoder returned a bare `std::vector<DbcProperty>` with no success/failure signal at all, so any bounds failure on a truncated or malformed memo silently returned whichever prefix had already decoded while `load_database_catalog_snapshot()` still reported the whole snapshot/export successful; (3) #5798, an unrecognized property type code's own value length is unknown by construction (the format has exactly six documented types, reverse-engineered from community analysis, with no separate length field to fall back on), yet the previous behavior stored the value's own first byte as hex and resumed parsing one byte later as a brand-new property header -- desynchronizing from the real property stream and risking fabricated bogus properties or dropped real ones past the first unrecognized entry. Fixed together since (2) and (3) share the same control flow (a structured decode result is the prerequisite for failing closed on an unrecognized type, not just on a bounds failure): `decode_dbc_properties_blob()` now returns a `DbcPropertiesDecodeResult {ok, properties, error}`; every bounds failure and every unrecognized type code fails the whole decode (and thus the whole catalog snapshot and every exporter that shares it) closed with a new localized diagnostic naming the byte offset, instead of silently truncating or guessing. The DateTime case is now decoded the same way `decode_value()`'s own `'T'` case in `dbf_table.cpp` decodes a table-level DateTime field -- two little-endian 32-bit components (Julian day count, milliseconds since midnight) -- reusing that exact `"julian:<N> millis:<M>"` text representation for consistency rather than inventing a second convention; a `(0, 0)` pair is VFP's own blank/null DateTime storage (matching the table-level writer's own null-DateTime encoding) and decodes to an empty value, mirroring this codebase's established "blank field displays as empty string" convention. Full codepage-aware handling of every conceivable future property type remains out of scope, as does this issue's own broader completion-criteria ask for documentation updates beyond this decoder's own header comment. New regression tests (`test_export_database_as_json_decodes_datetime_property`, `test_export_database_as_json_fails_closed_on_truncated_properties_memo`, `test_export_database_as_json_fails_closed_on_unsupported_property_type`) verified to reliably fail against the pre-fix code (6 total assertion failures across all three) and reliably pass against the fix -- the two binary-content fixtures (DateTime, unsupported type) needed a raw `.dct` memo-block byte patch rather than the ordinary `replace_record_field_value()` API, since that API validates its `value` argument as UTF-8 text round-tripped through the table's code page and cannot carry arbitrary binary property content. A PR review round (chatgpt-codex-connector, P2) caught that the new fail-closed behavior applied unconditionally to every catalog row, including a *deleted* one -- but a deleted row's own PROPERTIES memo, however corrupted, is never actually surfaced to anything (deleted rows are excluded from both table resolution and catalog serialization elsewhere in this same function), so a stale memo pointer on a logically-deleted row could make an otherwise exportable, live database unusable. Fixed by skipping PROPERTIES decoding entirely for a deleted row (`!raw.deleted` added to the existing guard) rather than decoding corrupted, never-used data only to discard it. New regression test `test_export_database_as_json_ignores_corrupt_properties_on_deleted_row` proves a truncated memo on a deleted row does not fail the export, while the live row's own data still exports correctly. #5827 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass against the merged #5743 fix itself): that fix's `is_valid_utf8()` field-name check rejected every valid legacy code-page-encoded name outright (the issue's own reproduction: a Windows-1252 field named "CAFé", whose raw byte `0xE9` is not valid standalone UTF-8) instead of decoding it through the table's own DBF header `code_page_mark` -- the exact convention `decode_dbf_text()` already established for field *values* (see e.g. `decode_value()`'s own `'C'` case in `dbf_table.cpp`). Fixed by adding `decode_dbf_table_field_names_in_place()`, called once per writer right after parsing: a name already valid UTF-8 is left untouched (the common case); an invalid one is decoded through `decode_dbf_text(tbl.table.header.code_page_mark, ...)` and, on success, the decoded UTF-8 replaces it everywhere it's reachable through the parsed table -- both the field descriptor's own name and every record value's own copy of it (`DbfTableParseResult::records[].values[].field_name`, copied from the same raw source at parse time) -- so no other emission site needed a separate change; failure (a name genuinely undecodable even through its own declared code page) still fails the whole export closed exactly as #5743 established. Replaces the previous 6 scattered per-writer `is_valid_utf8(fld.name)` checks with one shared, single-call-site function. New regression test `test_export_database_family_decodes_legacy_code_page_field_name` (the issue's own CP1252 reproduction, both JSON and portable SQL) verified to reliably fail against the pre-fix code and reliably pass against the fix. A further PR review round (chatgpt-codex-connector, P2) caught that this initial fix's own "is it already valid UTF-8?" fast path (skipping `decode_dbf_text()` entirely whenever the raw bytes already parsed as valid UTF-8) was itself unsound whenever a table declares a nonzero legacy code page: valid-UTF-8-shaped bytes are not the same thing as bytes actually *meant* as UTF-8 -- CP1252 bytes `0xC3 0xA9` are valid standalone UTF-8 for U+00E9 ('é'), but under a declared CP1252 mark they are two separate CP1252 characters, 'Ã' (U+00C3) and '©' (U+00A9); the fast path would have silently exported the wrong identifier for exactly this input, and could even collide with a different field whose name genuinely decodes to the preserved spelling. Fixed by removing the fast path entirely: every field name is now routed through `decode_dbf_text()` unconditionally, regardless of whether the raw bytes happen to already look like valid UTF-8 -- `decode_dbf_text()` itself already handles `code_page_mark == 0` as UTF-8 compatibility mode (effectively validating already-UTF-8 input), so this adds no special-casing and removes the ambiguity outright. New regression test `test_export_database_family_decodes_code_page_field_name_shaped_like_utf8` (the reviewer's own CP1252-bytes-that-look-like-UTF-8 example) verified to reliably fail against the pre-review-round code and reliably pass against the fix. **Scope note:** collision detection between two distinct raw names that decode to the identical UTF-8 string is not implemented (a pre-existing, more general gap this codebase has no field-name collision detector for today); index-metadata and import-round-trip consistency are not separately re-verified, since import always writes fresh UTF-8-encoded names (`decode_dbf_text()`'s own "no code-page mark" compatibility mode) that this same function already passes through unchanged (as `code_page_mark == 0`) on any subsequent re-export; per-code-page encode/decode correctness itself (CP1252, OEM, DBCS, etc.) is not re-tested here since `decode_dbf_text()` already has its own dedicated test coverage (`test_dbf_text_encoding.cpp`) -- this fix only proves the field-name export path correctly wires that existing, already-tested function in. | `defined` |

| `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-002` | Explicit repository-owner product policy in #140 that schema/table/relation interchange planning precedes data movement; derived from `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-001` and `HZ-data-corruption-01` | Copperfin shall provide a bounded native planning API that parses a valid schema-version-1 `EXPORT DATABASE ... TYPE JSON` envelope once, models its database name and DBF-writer-admissible table field descriptors deterministically, retains catalog and record payloads as inert JSON, preserves the exporter's empty-field marker as a no-schema table plan, and rejects malformed structure, unsupported schema versions, structurally invalid field descriptors, unsupported DBF storage types or widths, out-of-range numeric field values, and case-folded table or field-name collisions. The boundary shall never interpret the source-path field as authority, create or open an output path, invoke a provider, expose PRG `IMPORT DATABASE` syntax, or create, replace, overwrite, or mutate a DBC, DBF, memo, or index file. | `include/copperfin/platform/json.h`; `src/platform/json.cpp`; `include/copperfin/vfp/dbf_table.h`; `src/vfp/dbf_table.cpp`; `include/copperfin/vfp/asset_inspector.h`; `src/vfp/asset_inspector.cpp`; `cf_vfp_assets` | `tests/test_platform_models.cpp`; `tests/test_vfp_assets.cpp`; broader protected native validation remains release evidence | Focused Release tests prove one parsed bounded-document selection surface, successful deterministic plan construction, exporter-compatible empty-field planning, unsupported-schema, case-folded table collision, writer-incompatible DBF type/width rejection, invalid DBF-field, and oversized decimal rejection without any file reconstruction. | `HZ-data-corruption-01`; owner-policy modernization only, not VFP9 parity. Target layout, catalog/relation reconstruction, row materialization, external providers, overwrite consent, transactions, rollback/recovery, import syntax, and database-file mutation remain unimplemented and forbidden at this boundary. | `defined` |

| `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-003` | Explicit repository-owner product policy in #140, scoped as issue #5471; derived from `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-001` | Copperfin shall provide the non-VFP-extension command `EXPORT DATABASE <quoted-dbc-path> TO <quoted-output-path> TYPE SQL`, sharing `EXPORT DATABASE ... TYPE JSON`'s DBC catalog/table-resolution loader so the two `TYPE` variants cannot silently disagree on which tables/rows constitute the database. It shall resolve relative paths from the current default directory, append `.sql` only when the destination has no extension, emit `runtime.export_database_sql` only after a successful write, and preserve `export_database_command` as its shared package-debug IR opcode with `TYPE JSON`. It shall emit one portable/ANSI-ish SQL dialect: double-quoted identifiers, single-quoted string literals with embedded-quote doubling, `CREATE TABLE` per table mapping DBF field types to SQL column types (`VARCHAR` for character, `DECIMAL(length, decimals)` for numeric/float/currency, `INTEGER`, `DOUBLE PRECISION`, `BOOLEAN`, `DATE`, `TIMESTAMP`, and `TEXT` as the fallback for memo/general/picture pointer types whose content this loader does not resolve, matching the JSON exporter's own existing limitation there), followed by `INSERT` statements per row. Unsupported syntax/type, inspection failure, output-open failure, and output-write failure shall stop the PRG with localized diagnostics. | `include/copperfin/vfp/asset_inspector.h`; `src/vfp/asset_inspector.cpp`; `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl`; `resources/locales/*/strings.json` | `tests/test_prg_engine_data_io_import_export.cpp`; `tests/test_vfp_assets.cpp`; broader protected native validation remains release evidence | Focused `test_prg_engine_data_io` proves the `.sql` extension default, the CREATE TABLE/INSERT shape, VARCHAR/DECIMAL type mapping, quoted-vs-unquoted value formatting, and the `runtime.export_database_sql` event. Focused `test_vfp_assets` proves `export_database_as_sql()` shares `export_database_as_json()`'s missing-DBC-path error and, per #5475's review (found while adding the sibling Access-dialect exporter, applied here too for consistency), that a skipped table's name/error are sanitized the same way the header comment already is and that a numeric field's decoded value is only ever emitted unquoted when it is a safe plain decimal literal (`looks_like_safe_unquoted_sql_numeric_literal()`, `src/vfp/asset_inspector.cpp`), becoming `NULL` otherwise rather than trusting an overflow marker or crafted content unescaped. #5696 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass): the shared row writer had no `D`/Date branch at all, so a blank VFP date cell fell through to the generic string path and was emitted as an invalid `''` literal into a column declared `DATE`; fixed to emit `NULL` instead, matching the pattern already applied to the SQL Server/Oracle/MySQL writers, with a new regression test (`test_export_database_as_sql_family_preserves_blank_dates_as_null`) covering this exporter and its PostgreSQL/SQLite siblings that share this same row writer. | `HZ-data-corruption-01`; owner-policy modernization only, not VFP9 parity. No dialect-specific targeting (PostgreSQL/SQL Server/Oracle quoting or type differences), no memo-content resolution beyond what the shared loader already provides, and no `IMPORT DATABASE` of any kind implemented or claimed here. A destination resolving to the same file as the source is rejected at the shared dispatch layer before any read/write -- see `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-006`'s row for the full description of that shared guard. #5697 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass): a cataloged member DBF whose field-descriptor block parses "successfully" with zero fields (an aligned terminator immediately after the header, a real shape `parse_dbf_field_descriptor_block()` accepts) previously let `write_sql_tables_and_data()` (the shared row writer behind this exporter and `TYPE POSTGRESQL`/`TYPE SQLITE`) emit invalid `CREATE TABLE "name" ( );` DDL and still report `ok=true`. Fixed by adding a `hard_failure_error` out-parameter to `write_sql_tables_and_data()` (mirroring the established pattern in `write_oracle_tables_and_data()`/`write_mysql_tables_and_data()`) that fails the whole export closed with a new localized `Vfp.AssetInspector.Validation.ExportTableHasNoFields` diagnostic naming the offending table, applied identically across all eight `EXPORT DATABASE` variants (JSON, this portable SQL, Access, PostgreSQL, SQLite, SQL Server, Oracle, MySQL). The source-level asset inspector (`validate_dbf_field_descriptors()`) was also fixed to report this same zero-field shape as a validation error (`dbf.field_count_zero`) rather than returning silently with no diagnostic at all. The JSON import planner (`build_database_json_import_plan()`) was deliberately left accepting a `"fields": []` table entry -- that exact shape is also this exporter's own established marker for a cataloged table whose source `.dbf` could not be parsed at all (`test_database_json_import_plan_admits_exporter_unreadable_table_marker`, pre-existing), and since this fix means Copperfin's own `TYPE JSON` export can no longer emit that marker for a genuinely zero-field *parseable* table, the shape is now unambiguous in any JSON this codebase's own exporter produces. New regression tests (`test_export_database_family_fails_closed_on_zero_field_table`, covering all eight exporters against one raw-byte zero-field DBF fixture built directly since `create_dbf_table_file()` itself already rejects an empty field list on the write side; `test_inspect_asset_reports_zero_field_dbf_as_error`) verified to reliably fail against the pre-fix code and reliably pass against the fix. #5698 (found by an automated Codex code-review pass): the shared numeric branch previously substituted `NULL` for any non-blank value `looks_like_safe_unquoted_sql_numeric_literal()` rejected (a numeric-overflow marker, malformed fixed-width numeric text, a nonfinite binary Double, or crafted/corrupted content) -- silently changing the source data and reporting the migration successful, indistinguishable in the generated script from a genuinely blank/null cell. Fixed by distinguishing the two cases: a genuinely blank cell (empty `display_value`) still emits `NULL`; a non-blank value that fails the safety check instead fails the whole export closed with a new localized `Vfp.AssetInspector.Validation.UnsafeNumericValue` diagnostic naming the table, row, and column, applied identically across the portable SQL/PostgreSQL/SQLite writer, Access, SQL Server, Oracle, and MySQL. New regression tests (`test_export_database_family_fails_closed_on_unsafe_numeric_value`, covering the six non-Access exporters against one overflow-marker DBF fixture; `test_export_database_as_sql_still_preserves_blank_numeric_as_null`, proving the genuinely-blank case is unaffected) verified to reliably fail against the pre-fix code and reliably pass against the fix. The pre-existing `test_export_database_as_access_sql_rejects_unsafe_numeric_token` regression, which previously asserted the old NULL-substitution behavior, was updated to assert the new fail-closed behavior instead. | `defined` |

| `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-004` | Explicit repository-owner product policy in #140, scoped as issue #5472; derived from `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-002` and `HZ-data-corruption-01` | Copperfin shall provide the non-VFP-extension command `IMPORT DATABASE <quoted-json-path> TO <quoted-dbc-path> TYPE JSON`, reusing `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-002`'s existing planning/validation boundary unchanged and performing only the materialization step that boundary is deliberately forbidden from doing. It shall resolve relative paths from the current default directory and append `.dbc` only when the destination has no extension, matching the export commands' own convention. Before writing anything, it shall fail closed if the destination DBC path already exists or if any table's derived `<table_name>.dbf` path (alongside the DBC) already exists -- a duplicate case-folded table name in the plan is also rejected (defense in depth; `RQ-CF-MODERNIZATION-002`'s own planning boundary already rejects this earlier). It shall stage every file (each table DBF, then the DBC catalog) in a freshly created private directory on the destination volume, verify each write, and only then publish every staged file into its final destination with an exclusive hard link -- tables before the catalog, so a reader can never observe a catalog referencing a not-yet-existing table. TYPE JSON` snapshot re-imported via `IMPORT DATABASE ... TYPE JSON` into a fresh location, read back through `export_database_as_json()` and shown to match on table name, character/numeric/logical row data across multiple rows, the `runtime.import_database_json` event, destination-already-exists and table-path-collision fail-closed behavior (including that a rejected re-import does not modify the already-materialized files), and the quoted-path-only syntax contract.
