## LLR-VFP-NUM-001 Evidence Boundary

The shipped help directly establishes two facts: VFP numeric/calculation syntax
uses a decimal point, and changing the displayed point does not change the
period required in calculations. The host-locale isolation clause is the
portable implementation consequence of those facts: Copperfin must not let the
embedding C/C++ locale reinterpret the VFP token grammar.

This row does **not** require displayed numeric text to ignore `SET POINT`,
`SET SYSFORMATS`, or other VFP formatting state. It also does not claim that all
string-to-number functions share source-literal syntax. Those surfaces require
separate recovered requirements and evidence.

The shared double parser requires full input consumption, rejects leading
whitespace, malformed signs, comma decimals, trailing text, overflow, and
nonfinite values unless a consuming binary-field contract explicitly opts in.
On Apple libc++, where floating-point `std::from_chars` may be unavailable, the
fallback stream is explicitly imbued with `std::locale::classic()` and uses
`std::noskipws`, preserving the same invariant contract.

Independent Linux review at channel sequence 1419 fetched and checked both
shipped-help pages, verified every mapped code and test location, and ran the
actual `test_prg_engine_parser_classes` and `test_prg_engine_data_io` targets.
The latter is the CMake target containing
`test_prg_engine_data_io_save_restore.cpp`; the source shard is not a standalone
test target. The Linux host's missing `pt_BR` and `de_DE` locales are disclosed
rather than inferred as executions. Linux source review confirmed the mapped
comma-decimal parser test constructs its locale facet in process, while the
macOS matrix supplies both installed locale runs.

## LLR-VFP-NUM-002 Evidence Boundary

The requirement text is recovered from the mounted shipped CHM, not from
Copperfin behavior. No binary was decompiled or disassembled. A temporary
current-head probe was used only to compare the recovered requirement with the
implementation: it produced `1.234,5000` for a numeric value but invariant
`1234.5000` for Currency under comma `POINT` and period `SEPARATOR`, proving a
display-path gap before #4914 changed product code.

The correction formats `value_as_string()` output for Currency through the
existing display-symbol seam. That source text is produced directly from the
signed scaled `int64` magnitude and four decimal digits, so display formatting
does not convert through `double`. The same seam serves runtime print and
no-picture `TRANSFORM()`. It deliberately does not add the `SET CURRENCY`
symbol: symbol presence and position remain picture/output-surface behavior,
outside this recovered `SET POINT` requirement.

`value_as_string(Currency)`, calculation parsing, SAVE/RESTORE, manifests,
JSON, and other machine-readable paths remain invariant period-decimal under
`LLR-VFP-NUM-001`. The focused regressions separately prove default period,
explicit punctuation, argument-free reset, per-data-session isolation, exact
four-place Currency digits, and unchanged invariant stringification.

The macOS locale evidence runs the two mapped targets from the shared native
validation action after the full suite. It is guarded by the invariant
`macos` platform input so Linux and Windows behavior is unchanged, while the
platform workflows remain shell-free thin callers. Exact run `31281606203`
checked out `0af4807ca` and passed all eight focused executions.

| `RQ-CF-PRG-042` | Installed VFP9 SP2 differential probe retained at `/home/rich/temp/vfp9-probes/bare-return-6442/` (VFP9 output `type=L;value=.T.` for both a bare and an implicit return) and Copperfin's own retained pre-fix probe at `/home/rich/temp/copperfin-bare-return-probe`; issue #6442 | An explicit bare `RETURN` (no expression) and an implicit `RETURN` performed when a `FUNCTION`/`PROCEDURE`/`METHOD` reaches its end without executing any `RETURN` statement shall both yield logical `.T.`, not an empty value. `last_return_value` is session-wide and is never reset between calls, so a routine that falls off the end must have this default forced at the moment its frame is popped rather than silently inheriting whatever value an earlier, unrelated `RETURN` left behind. `RETURN TO MASTER`/`RETURN TO ProcedureName` (added by #6441) also default to logical `.T.` for consistency, since they likewise carry no expression. Review-round fix: the implicit default is scoped to every frame except the true outermost/root session frame (not merely `procedure_context` frames, since `push_main_frame()` is reused for both the session's root and an ordinary nested `DO child.prg` call -- a nested program-file call reaching its own end gets the same default as a routine call), and is suppressed entirely for a frame being forcibly unwound (a fault propagating to an enclosing `TRY...CATCH`, `CANCEL`, `RETURN TO`) rather than one that genuinely ran out of statements, since `execute_current_statement()` advances a frame's program counter past a statement before dispatching it, so a routine's own last statement failing already looks "exhausted" by `pc` alone. | `src/runtime/prg_engine_flow.inl` (`pop_frame(bool natural_completion)`, root-frame exclusion); Full suite run surfaced a real regression in the runtime host's bridge-invocation mechanism (`test_runtime_host_debug_output_formatting`), which reads `last_return_value` after running a synthetic `DO Export` bootstrap script and depends on the bootstrap's own top-level completion not overwriting the exported routine's already-captured return value. This is a forced abort, not a normal return: by-reference parameter writeback into the caller is suppressed during the unwind, and the implicit-return-defaults-to-logical-true behavior (`RQ-CF-PRG-042`) does not apply to a forcibly unwound frame. Review-round fix: `release_frame_object_bindings()` itself only scanned `frame.locals`/`frame.local_arrays`, so a PRIVATE-declared object -- which lives directly in `globals` for the declaring frame's lifetime, with only the shadowed prior value kept in `frame.private_saved_values` for `restore_private_declarations()` to restore afterward -- was never discovered or released, on CANCEL or on an ordinary frame return alike. `release_frame_object_bindings()` now also scans each PRIVATE name's current global/array value before it is restored, using the same `release_object_memory_binding()`/`release_memory_binding()` helpers already used for LOCAL bindings. | `src/runtime/prg_engine_flow.inl` (`pop_frame(bool natural_completion, bool sync_byref)`, adds a `sync_byref` suppression parameter; `release_frame_object_bindings()` extended to scan PRIVATE bindings via `frame.private_saved_values`/`private_saved_arrays`); `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`cancel_statement` dispatch, unwind loop routed through `pop_frame(false, false)`) | `tests/test_prg_engine_control_flow_routine_calls_set_procedure.cpp` (`test_cancel_releases_frame_owned_native_objects`, `test_cancel_releases_frame_owned_private_native_objects`) | Focused portable CTest `test_prg_engine_control_flow` reproduces the issue's exact trigger (a `LOCAL` native object created in a called procedure, followed by `CANCEL`) and proves `Destroy` runs, `prg.object.destroy`/`prg.object.release` events are emitted, and a global set from inside `Destroy` is observably true; verified fail-then-pass against a reverted implementation (all three assertions failed exactly as before the fix, matching the issue's own reported absence of these events). A review round then caught that the fix (and its changelog claim) covered LOCAL but not PRIVATE bindings; a second test (`test_cancel_releases_frame_owned_private_native_objects`) reproduces the same trigger with a `PRIVATE` object, verified fail-then-pass the same way. A Copilot review round found three further gaps: (1) `current_record_matches_visibility()`'s own internal filter-then-FOR double evaluation was unprotected by the outer generation check, since the outer check only runs after the whole call returns; (2) `synchronize_relations_for_parent()` itself evaluates arbitrary relation expressions against the parent and was unchecked, so a relation callback closing the parent left `replace_current_record_fields()` reporting success while its caller kept using the freed cursor; (3) a multi-record scoped `REPLACE` was asked whether it could report failure while having already committed earlier records. The code fix for both is nonetheless a direct, minimal, clearly-correct response to the specific reviewer-identified reentrancy gap, verified functionally (both tests demonstrably require the fix to pass their catchable-error/data-preserved assertions) even though a memory-corruption reproduction specifically was not achieved for these two. A Copilot review round on PR #6481 found five further gaps (relation-key match expressions unprotected in `synchronize_skip_parent_for_child()`'s own matching calls; a closed walked parent and a failed trailing sync both silently discarded instead of propagated; registration not atomic with respect to a *later* synchronization failure) -- three new targeted regression tests were added and fail-then-pass verified: the match-expression-closes-parent case reproduced genuine Valgrind-confirmed corruption (38 errors, invalid read inside `move_by_visible_records()` reached through the freed parent); the walked-parent-closure and registration-rollback cases were verified at the assertion level (disabling only the propagation/rollback fix did not itself crash, since the underlying internal protection already prevented that -- it surfaced a silent wrong-success instead, exactly the reviewer's concern). `test_prg_engine_table_mutation` (396 tests) and `test_prg_engine_control_flow` continue to pass unchanged, and the full suite (396 tests) passes after the review round. | `HZ-runtime-crash-01`; `HZ-data-corruption-01`; this is the fifth issue closed from the ~18-issue reentrant-cursor-closure cluster (after #6243, #6244, #6245, #6246). Explicitly out of scope and not addressed here, per the issue's own broader acceptance criteria: full `ASan`/Valgrind coverage across cycles, nested (3+ level) relation chains, `SET RELATION OFF` interactions with a mid-evaluation closure, and order-expression side effects during relation-key evaluation (order/index expressions can themselves invoke UDFs in principle, sharing surface with #6269's `SET FILTER` scope and general index-expression reentrancy, not investigated here). `move_by_visible_records()`'s own `SET FILTER`-expression evaluation during the one-to-many parent walk is a related, separately-tracked reentrancy surface (#6269), not hardened by this fix. | `recovered` |
| `RQ-CF-PRG-057` | Retained differential probe evidence and Valgrind logs described in the issue itself and reproduced independently (`SET FILTER TO (DropCursor()) IN people` where `DropCursor()` does `USE IN people`: the uninstrumented run reports `completed=1` with the closure invisible, while Valgrind reports invalid reads and writes through freed `CursorState` storage -- reproduced here as 16 errors across 12 contexts, an invalid string-copy read reached through the freed cursor's `filter_expression` field); issue #6269. The issue's own VFP9/Wine comparison is decisive here: real VFP9 does **not** raise an error when a parenthesized `SET FILTER` expression closes its own target -- the command simply completes (`success;used=.F.`) and a later `USED()` correctly reports the closure, unlike every other issue in this cluster (#6243-#6247) where VFP-compatible behavior is a catchable error | `SET FILTER`'s filter-expression evaluation may run arbitrary VFP code, including `USE IN`/`CLOSE ALL` on the exact work area receiving the filter. Copperfin shall never write to or read from that cursor afterward through a stale reference. Consistent with VFP9's own observed behavior (not a data-integrity violation the way a fabricated result or corrupted table would be, per [[feedback_copperfin_vfp9_parity_not_absolute]]-equivalent reasoning applied here), the command shall still complete successfully -- skipping the now-pointless write to the closed cursor's `filter_expression` -- rather than inventing a new catchable-error path that would diverge from VFP9's own graceful behavior. | `src/runtime/prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (the `normalized_name == "filter"` branch of `StatementKind::set_option`/`SET`: captures a `CursorGenerationReference` for the resolved target cursor immediately before evaluating the filter-clause expression; after evaluation, re-resolves the cursor by generation identity and only writes `filter_expression` if it is still alive; the `runtime.filter` event's detail is now built from the local `filter_clause` string rather than re-reading it off the possibly-freed cursor, so the event is emitted correctly either way) | `tests/test_prg_engine_seek_index_cursor_state_02.cpp` (`test_set_filter_expression_closing_target_cursor_completes_gracefully`) | Focused portable CTest `test_prg_engine_seek_index` reproduces the issue's exact `SET FILTER TO (dropcursor()) IN People` trigger and proves: the script completes without crashing; `USED('People')` correctly reports `.F.` afterward; script execution continues normally past the `SET FILTER` line (no catchable error, matching VFP9); and the `runtime.filter` event is still emitted. Verified fail-then-pass with genuine Valgrind-confirmed memory corruption: 16 errors across 12 contexts (an invalid read reconstructing a string from the freed cursor's `filter_expression` field, reached from the event-detail construction) with the guard reverted, cleared to 0 errors with the guard restored. All portable tests pass. | `HZ-runtime-crash-01`; `HZ-data-corruption-01`; this is the sixth issue closed from the ~18-issue reentrant-cursor-closure cluster (after #6243, #6244, #6245, #6246, #6247), and the first where the correct VFP-compatible response is graceful non-error completion rather than a catchable error -- worth remembering when picking up remaining cluster issues: check each one's own VFP9-comparison evidence before defaulting to the catchable-error pattern established by the majority of this cluster's fixes. `move_by_visible_records()`'s own filter-expression evaluation during ordinary navigation (`GO`/`SKIP`/`SEEK`, and the one-to-many `SET SKIP` parent walk from #6247) is explicitly out of scope here -- this fix covers only `SET FILTER`'s own installation-time evaluation, not every place a cursor's `filter_expression` is later evaluated. | `recovered` |
| `RQ-CF-PRG-058` | Retained differential probe evidence and Valgrind logs described in the issue itself and reproduced independently (`SELECT NAME FROM People WHERE dropcursor() INTO ARRAY result` where `dropcursor()` does `USE IN People`: the native run reported a fabricated runtime out-of-memory fault; a projection-callback variant hung until its process bound expired; Valgrind reported extensive invalid reads/writes into the freed `CursorState` map node in both -- reproduced here as 535 errors across 49 contexts, invalid reads inside `evaluate_expression()`/`capture_cursor_expression_reference()` reached through the freed cursor during row materialization); issue #6251, the disclosed follow-on from #6246's own review round, which found that `materialize_select_query_rows()` delegates entirely to the general-purpose `requery_native_list_control()`/`build_rows_from_query_plan()` SELECT execution engine, shared by direct `SELECT ... INTO ARRAY`/`INTO CURSOR`, `INSERT INTO ... SELECT`, and native listbox/grid `.Requery()` -- none of which had any reentrancy protection | `SELECT` query row materialization (visibility-filter, `WHERE`, projection, `GROUP BY`, `HAVING`, `ORDER BY`, join-on, and the join/left-join per-row and per-group evaluation loops) shall never continue touching the source or joined cursor through a stale reference after any of those arbitrary-code expressions closed or replaced it; a closure shall raise the existing catchable `{command}`-style failure already used by `materialize_select_query_rows()`'s callers, and no partially-materialized rows/`_TALLY` update shall be published as success. The pre-existing, disclosed bare aggregate-function-call scanning gap (`aggregate_function_value()`, `? SUM(...)`-style forms, #6243/#6245) and its `GROUP BY` analogue (`evaluate_group_aggregate()`'s own unprotected per-record scan, newly identified here) are explicitly out of scope -- both need a distinct fix mechanism (error propagation through expression evaluation, not statement/row-loop dispatch) and are left for a future, dedicated pass. | `src/runtime/prg_engine.cpp` (`build_rows_from_query_plan()`: added a non-defaulted `bool &cursor_lost` out-parameter; captured `CursorGenerationReference`s for both `cursor` and `joined_cursor` once, up front, alongside by-value-saved work areas so the trailing alias-restore logic never re-reads `.work_area` off a possibly-freed cursor; added `cursor_alive()`/`joined_cursor_alive()` checks after visibility-filter and every `WHERE`/projection/`GROUP BY`/`HAVING`/`ORDER BY`/join-on `evaluate_expression()` call across the grouped-query record-collection loop, the grouped-query output-construction loop, the main per-row loop's `materialize_current_row()` lambda, and the inner join sub-loop; guarded the `LEFT JOIN` unmatched-row synthesis block's structural restoration of `joined_cursor`'s fields on `joined_cursor_alive()`, since `materialize_current_row()` runs in between; guarded the trailing, universally-reached cleanup block (`restore_cursor_snapshot()` calls and alias-restore bookkeeping) on the same liveness checks, since it previously ran unconditionally regardless of which code path executed; INTO ARRAY` command path, and proves in each case: a catchable error is raised; `USED()` confirms genuine closure; the actual system `_TALLY` and the existing target array are preserved (including after one row was already materialized); and script execution continues normally afterward. Verified fail-then-pass with genuine Valgrind-confirmed memory corruption: 535 errors across 49 contexts with the `WHERE`/projection liveness guards temporarily reverted, cleared to 0 errors with them restored (verified against a trimmed build running only the two new tests, since the full `test_prg_engine_control_flow` suite is too large to run under Valgrind in a reasonable time). Full suite: 396 tests, 100% pass. | `HZ-runtime-crash-01`; `HZ-data-corruption-01`; this is the seventh issue closed from the ~18-issue reentrant-cursor-closure cluster (after #6243, #6244, #6245, #6246, #6247, #6269), and by far the largest single fix in it -- one shared, ~900-line query-materialization function used by three independent callers. Deliberately not fixed here, and disclosed above: `aggregate_function_value()`'s and the newly-identified `evaluate_group_aggregate()`'s own internal per-record scanning loops remain unprotected for bare/`GROUP BY` aggregate projections specifically; order/index-expression side effects share reentrancy surface with #6269's already-closed `SET FILTER` scope and general index-expression reentrancy, not investigated here. The `.Requery()` UI method call site was traced and confirmed to already treat a `false` return gracefully (no script-level exception), so no additional dispatch-level work was needed there. | `recovered` |
| `RQ-CF-PRG-059` | Repository-owner-authored, `agent-approved` issue #6047 ("nullable-field `_NullFlags` support"), scoped by explicit owner instruction to the on-disk storage layer only (declaration parsing through `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE CURSOR`, `REPLACE`, `COPY STRUCTURE EXTENDED`, and read-back); the `PrgValue`/`VARTYPE()`/`EMPTY()`/`NVL()`/`EVL()`/aggregate-on-NULL semantics redesign flagged during scoping is deliberately deferred to a separate follow-up issue, per explicit owner direction, and is NOT covered by this requirement. Byte-level layout (field-descriptor flags-byte bit `0x02`, hidden type-`'0'` `_NullFlags` bitmap field, one bit per nullable field in declaration order, LSB-first) recovered empirically from a real headless VFP9 9.0 SP2 instance under Wine (`docs/80-dbf-nullflags-field-format-notes.md`), not from any decompiled binary, per `docs/07-clean-room-rules.md`. | A `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE CURSOR` field declaration with an explicit `NULL` clause shall persist a physical nullability bit for that field: the table shall gain a hidden `_NullFlags` bitmap field (present only when at least one declared field is nullable) sized to one bit per nullable field, and each such field's descriptor flags byte shall record its own nullability. `REPLACE ... WITH .NULL.` shall set the corresponding bit and blank the field's storage; replacing a null field with a real value shall clear the bit. Reading a record back shall report `is_null` per field from the bitmap, authoritative over any value-based heuristic. The hidden `_NullFlags` field shall never appear in a cursor's user-visible field enumeration (`FIELD()`, `AFIELDS()`, `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE` field lists, `GETFLDSTATE()`/`SETFLDSTATE()` ordinal/`-1` forms, etc.), even though the underlying DBF parser's own physical field list is intentionally left unfiltered for schema-rewrite/exporter compatibility, and a schema rewrite (`ALTER TABLE`) on a table that already has a `_NullFlags` field shall never produce a second one. `COPY STRUCTURE EXTENDED`'s `FIELD_NULL` metadata column shall report the field's real nullability instead of an unconditional `"F"`, for both `CREATE TABLE`-declared and `ALTER TABLE ADD/ALTER COLUMN`-declared fields. A field declared with no `NULL`/`NOT NULL` clause at all shall NOT consume a bitmap bit (distinct from `TableFieldDeclaration.nullable`'s pre-existing, unrelated default-true meaning for NOT-NULL-constraint enforcement only). A buffered `REPLACE ... WITH .NULL.` shall persist as a real NULL through `TABLEUPDATE()`, not just in the in-memory buffer. The flags byte shall be decoded only for the 32-byte VFP descriptor layout, never for a legacy 16-byte FoxBASE descriptor (which has no flags byte at that offset at all). **Explicitly excluded from this requirement, disclosed rather than silently unhandled:** a memo-type (`M`/`G`/`P`) field's own NULL state (its bitmap bit is never set/cleared by a memo-field write); and a Character/Varchar/Varbinary (`C`/`V`/`Q`) field's NULL state when copied through the plain string-based `create_dbf_table_file()` API (e.g. `COPY TO`), since that API has no per-cell null channel and the text-sentinel convention is deliberately never applied to string types (a real value could legitimately be the literal text `"null"`). Both are pre-existing architectural limits of the string-based creation API, not regressions introduced by this requirement. | `include/copperfin/vfp/dbf_table.h`/`src/vfp/dbf_table.cpp` (`DbfFieldDescriptor`/`RawFieldDescriptor` gain `nullable`; new `apply_null_flag_bit()` helper; `create_dbf_table_file_with_memo_payloads()` writes descriptor flags byte 18 and appends a synthetic `_NullFlags` field, sized `ceil(nullable_count/8)`, after every real field -- appended after, not through, the existing `supports_table_field_storage()` per-field validation loop, so the synthetic field needs no carve-out there; `parse_dbf_field_descriptor_block()` reads the flags byte; `parse_dbf_table_from_file()` decodes the bitmap once per record and overrides each governed field's `is_null`, authoritative over `decode_value()`'s own heuristic, while leaving `table.fields` itself unfiltered; `replace_record_field_value_targeted()`/`_impl()` and the public `replace_record_field_value()`/`_additive()`/`_full_rewrite()`/`_additive_full_rewrite()` family gain an explicit `is_null` parameter, updating the record's `_NullFlags` byte(s) in the same in-memory buffer as the ordinary field write, with no extra I/O); `src/runtime/prg_engine_table_structure_helpers.h`/`.cpp` (`TableFieldDeclaration` gains `null_clause_specified`, set only when the declaration text literally contains `NULL`/`NOT NULL`; `table_field_descriptors()` sets `field.nullable = declaration.null_clause_specified && declaration.nullable`, keeping `CREATE TABLE FROM ARRAY`'s synthesized, clause-less declarations from silently consuming bitmap bits); `src/runtime/prg_engine_free_functions.inl` (new `visible_cursor_fields()` helper, filtering the hidden field out of a cursor's field list by type and `collapse_identifier()`-normalized name, applied at cursor construction rather than in the shared DBF parser); `src/runtime/prg_engine_session_transaction_recovery.inl`/`prg_engine_sql.inl` (`CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE CURSOR` cursor construction) and `prg_engine_dispatch.inl` (`ALTER TABLE` schema-reload) apply `visible_cursor_fields()` when populating `cursor.local_fields`, which `cursor_field_descriptors()` returns to its ~25 call sites across the runtime (aggregate, records, dispatch, session-loading, variables) -- filtering at the 3 population points rather than at each consumer; `prg_engine_dispatch.inl`'s `COPY STRUCTURE EXTENDED TO` reads `field.nullable` for `FIELD_NULL` instead of a hardcoded `"F"`; `src/runtime/prg_engine_records.inl` (`EvaluatedReplaceAssignment` gains `is_null`, threaded through both the buffered-update path -- fixing a separate, real bug where a buffered `REPLACE ... WITH .NULL.` was silently discarded before any `TABLEUPDATE()` -- and the immediate-write path, both now consulting `PrgValue.is_null` before falling through to ordinary string serialization); `src/vfp/asset_inspector.cpp` (Oracle exporter's `table_declares_nullable_fields` blank-value workaround removed, since `DbfRecordValue.is_null` is now authoritative). **Review-round fixes:** `read_raw_field_descriptors()` (the root-cause fix -- now decodes the flags byte, since this function, used by every `REPLACE` writer, is separate from the already-correct `parse_dbf_field_descriptor_block()`); `parse_dbf_field_descriptor_block()`'s flags-byte decode now guarded to the 32-byte VFP layout only (`layout.descriptor_size == 32U`), never the legacy 16-byte FoxBASE layout; `apply_null_flag_bit()` and the read-side `null_flags_field` lookup now match on name (`_NullFlags`, case-insensitive) as well as type; the read-side and create-time record-bitmap loops now use a `std::vector<std::uint8_t>` instead of a `std::uint16_t` accumulator; `rewrite_dbf_table_schema()` now filters any existing physical `_NullFlags` field out of the caller-supplied field list before rewriting (so `create_dbf_table_file_with_memo_payloads()`'s own unconditional fresh-bitmap-append never duplicates it), and `collect_dbf_rewrite_rows()` now substitutes the `"null"` text sentinel for a mapped non-string field whose source `is_null` was true, so the freshly-computed bitmap preserves real pre-rewrite NULL state instead of losing it; `prg_engine_dispatch.inl`'s `ALTER TABLE ADD/ALTER COLUMN` dispatch now builds its own `field.nullable` from `declaration->null_clause_specified && declaration->nullable` instead of passing the declaration's raw (always non-nullable) descriptor straight through; `prg_engine_free_functions.inl` gained `visible_record_values()` (mirrors `visible_cursor_fields()` for one record's already-decoded values) applied in `GETFLDSTATE()`/`SETFLDSTATE()`'s ordinal, `-1`, and name-lookup branches; `prg_engine_records.inl`'s three buffered-`TABLEUPDATE()`-flush call sites (mode-4/5 `commit_buffered_record()`, verified staging, mode-1) now pass `field.is_null` through to the writer instead of relying on its default. | `tests/test_prg_engine_table_structure.cpp` (`test_create_table_replace_with_null_persists_null_flag`, rewritten to look up fields by name after its original `values.size() == 4U` guard was found to never match; `test_alter_table_add_column_preserves_existing_null_flags_bitmap`, new); `tests/test_prg_engine_data_io_import_export.cpp` (`test_copy_structure_extended_reports_nullable_field_flag`); `tests/test_prg_engine_table_mutation_maintenance.cpp` (`test_table_buffered_replace_null_persists_through_tableupdate`, new); `tests/test_vfp_assets.cpp` (`test_export_database_as_oracle_sql_allows_blank_value_in_table_with_nullable_fields`, fixture updated to a real descriptor-flags-byte/`_NullFlags`-bearing table instead of relying on the removed workaround). | A dedicated general-purpose research agent first produced a full, independently-verified map of every touch point before implementation began. The first implementation pass built the correct byte layout and the correct high-level structure (cursor field-list filtering, `TableFieldDeclaration.null_clause_specified`, etc.), verified fail-then-pass for its own regression test -- but that test's own guard condition (`values.size() == 4U`) could never be satisfied, since a real record has 5 values (the unfiltered `_NullFlags` field included), so every one of its assertions silently never ran; the test reported a false "pass" while the actual write path (`apply_null_flag_bit()`, reached via `replace_record_field_value_targeted()`/`_impl()`) was completely non-functional, because the `RawFieldDescriptor` list it consulted (from `read_raw_field_descriptors()`, a function distinct from the correctly-fixed `parse_dbf_field_descriptor_block()`) never decoded the nullable flag at all, so every field's `nullable` stayed `false` and the bit-index lookup always failed silently. This shipped as an open PR and drew a Codex + Copilot review round, which independently found this root cause along with 10 further real defects: the schema-rewrite (`ALTER TABLE`) duplicate-`_NullFlags` bug described above; `_NullFlags` identified by type alone (collision risk with a preserved opaque type-`0` field); a `uint16_t` bitmap accumulator silently breaking past 16 nullable fields (reader and writer); `ALTER TABLE ADD/ALTER COLUMN` bypassing `table_field_descriptors()`'s null-clause logic entirely; the flags-byte offset being read unconditionally, misreading legacy FoxBASE's 16-byte descriptors; three separate buffered-`TABLEUPDATE()`-flush call sites still calling the writer with a hardcoded `is_null=false`; and `GETFLDSTATE()`/`SETFLDSTATE()`'s ordinal and `-1` forms indexing directly into the physical (unfiltered) record-values list. Every one of these was fixed and verified fail-then-pass individually (temporarily reverting each specific guard/fix and confirming the *intended* assertion -- not just some assertion -- fails, then restoring it), including a self-caught bug introduced by one of the fixes itself (`GETFLDSTATE`'s named-field branch computing `std::distance()` against the wrong container's iterator after the values list was filtered). Two new regression tests were added covering the schema-rewrite dedup + ALTER-TABLE-added-column NULL-bit case and the buffered-`TABLEUPDATE()` persistence case; the original test's guard was rewritten to look up fields by name rather than asserting an exact `values.size()`, so it can never again silently skip its own assertions. Regression sweep across 9 test binaries (`test_dbf_table`, `test_vfp_assets`, `test_prg_engine_table_structure`, `test_prg_engine_control_flow`, `test_prg_engine_data_io`, `test_prg_engine_table_mutation`, `test_prg_engine_seek_index`, `test_staged_import_publish`, `test_prg_engine_relations`) passed locally after the review-round fixes; full hosted CI validation pending this requirement's PR. | `recovered` |
